# Tasks: Full Project Audit and Remediation

**Input**: Design documents from `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation/` (spec.md, plan.md, research.md, data-model.md, contracts/, quickstart.md, docs/01-21)

**Prerequisites**: plan.md and spec.md (present), research.md (79 owner decisions, OD-01..OD-79), data-model.md, contracts/, docs/21 rev 8 (master plan: 8 phases P0-P7, 53 work packages, 13 streams; 40 grouped owner decisions ODG-01..ODG-40, of which ODG-39 (the docs/18 innovation intake) and ODG-40 (the licence policy) were added in docs/21 rev 6, plus 15 open ungrouped `research.md` decisions in its section 8.6, which lists 16 rows with the resolved OD-60)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Revision | 8 |
| Created | 2026-10-03 |
| Last modified | 2026-10-03 |
| Status | draft rev 8 - applies the round-7 independent reviews of P0-P3 and their P0-P3 cross-file needs under the plan owner's binding design decision for the commit-push script (CPA), which supersedes the rev 7 design of script-owned paths and an intake of pending outputs: CPA never writes into the tracked tree and never commits its own outputs; every output of a run (report, summary, verifier JSON, transcripts, deferral rows) stays in the ignored run directory `.audit/commit-push/<run_id>/` under a unique run id; the durable record of a run is git itself (the `CPA-Run:`, `Deferred-Gates:`, `Awaits-Review:` and `Foreign-Commit:` lines of the commits it creates), and a task that needs a run's report captures it through the evidence recorder as an ordinary member of its own change set (CPA run record); shared stores such as `$EV/deferrals.jsonl` and `$EV/blobs/` are ordinary members of the change set of whoever wrote them; a run that does not get the lock writes only its own run directory; `--repo` commits record pending pin moves in `.audit/pending_pins.tsv`; a held commit (`Awaits-Review:`) is pushed only once its review verdict is GO; the declared change set is an explicit path list (`--paths-from`); clean-tree gates measure the clean tracked tree of the abbreviation table (T039-T047, T022, T029, T080, T085, T090, T093, T095, T161, T178, T225, T300, T302); the no-pipeline check has its single home in S3 (`check_no_ci.sh`, AM-G2 reusing it, T040, T090) and `.secrets.baseline` its single owner T040a (shrink only); plus commit windows for parallel streams, the `/out` rule for container outputs (RUNP `--out`), the revision-header convention for new Markdown files, the ratchet tightening rule, the append-only evidence-store check, the in-tree blob exemption from the large-file check, pinned `detect-secrets`, `pre-commit-hooks` and `goimports` (T006, T008, T106), the docling exception row (T032), the `.claude/` hook-output accounting (T082), the T212 caller list, the F-INDEX-004 scope (T027), the regenerated bank-id floor through `/out` (T214), `$EVID` renamed `$CPA_RUN` (docs/08 §6.1 uses `$EVID` for another folder), the task-id citation check T283a (docs/21 §12.7), and the open state-vector question of the repeat audit run recorded as UNCONFIRMED in T300; ids T001-T595 stay frozen; pending re-review. Rev 7 applied the round-6 reviews of P0-P3 |
| Task ids | 595 frozen ids T001-T595 (no renumbering from rev 7 on) plus suffix-id tasks placed where they belong, no existing id ever changed; added in rev 7: T040a, T042a, T248a (P0-P3), T434a, T462a, T580a (P4-P7); added in rev 8: T283a (P3), T595a, T595b (P7); 604 tasks in all |
| Derived from | docs/21 section 5 work-package catalogue (every WP deliverable and acceptance item) |

**Tests**: Mandatory. This feature's spec and constitution (11.4.224, 11.4.27, 11.4.169) require test-first work with the RED run observed before implementation and every PASS backed by machine-produced evidence. Tasks marked `[TDD]` name the RED condition.

**Organization (deviation from the template, stated)**: the template groups phases by user story. This feature is an audit-and-remediation programme whose work packages cut across all seven user stories, and docs/21 fixes the execution order by dependency (P0 foundation to P7 closure). The phases below therefore follow docs/21 P0-P7; every task carries its `[USn]` tag so a story-ordered view is a filter, not a re-plan. Phase P0 plays the template's Setup role, P1-P2 its Foundational role (no audit or fix work starts before P1 exit and the P2 baselines), and P3-P7 carry the user-story work (US1 register, US2 audit, US3 fixes and evidence, US4 documentation, US5 per-application coverage, US6 dependencies, US7 commit and push hygiene).

## Format: `- [ ] Tnnn [P] [USn] [markers] Description with exact path`

- **[P]**: parallel-safe (different files, no unfinished dependency)
- **[USn]**: user story from spec.md (US1..US7)
- **[TDD]**: RED-GREEN-REFACTOR, RED observed on the broken artifact before implementation
- **[REVIEW]**: independent Opus-xhigh review gate (11.4.142/11.4.209), reviewer stream separate from producer (11.4.240)
- **[SUBAGENT]**: can be delegated to a subagent
- **BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn**: waits for the owner decision (doc21 section 8, research.md OD ids); the safe default is stated where research.md defines one
- Paths marked `(new)` do not exist yet and were chosen by the plan; the reviewer confirms them. In every phase, a path that a task creates and that does not exist at the plan commit is a deliverable of the task that names it, whether or not it carries the mark (the phase-group blocks restate this)

## Constraints that bind every task

Rootless Podman containers only, builds distributed via the containers submodule (11.4.173; until the owner answers ODG-07, images are built and containers run on the local host as a tracked deviation of the P0-P1 conventions, never claimed compliant); no CI/CD (11.4.156); no force-push, ff-only to all upstreams (11.4.113); all work on main branches (FR-024); no credential value in any file (11.4.10); a real service or device is required for every non-unit check, and when it is absent the result is `blocked-unavailable`, never a pass (spec clarification A2); every fix leaves a RED, three GREEN, a caught mutation and an independent GO review (docs/04, docs/06).

---
## Shared abbreviations (apply to every phase)

| Abbreviation | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `$FEAT` = `$SPEC` | `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation` |
| `$EV` | `$FEAT/evidence` (ledger.jsonl of `ev/1` entries, anchors, blobs, verdicts; docs/06 section 11); fixed subfolders `reviews/` (every `[REVIEW]` verdict), `hc/` (human checkpoints), `coverage_baseline/<app>/` and `coverage_targets/<app>/` (never a folder named `coverage/`, which `.gitignore:139` ignores at any depth); every other `$EV/<name>/` folder or `$EV/<name>.json` file is owned by the task that first writes it, which names it |
| `$CPA_RUN` | the run directory of one CPA run, `.audit/commit-push/<run_id>/` (ignored by the T004 `/.audit/` rule, never tmpfs, so a crashed run leaves its stage files for the anti-mess sweep; named `$EVID` until rev 8, renamed because docs/08 §6.1 uses `$EVID` for `$EV/web`); `<run_id>` is the UTC start time to the second, the process id and a random suffix (for example `20261003T120000Z-41234-9f3ac2e1`), so two runs never share one; every output of the run lives there and nowhere else: `report.json` (written last, holding the sha256 of every other file of the directory), `summary.txt`, the stage transcripts, the S7 verifier JSON `verify.json`, the deferral rows, the `/out` directories of the run's container checks and its disk-headroom records (T042); the only file outside it that CPA writes is the ignored `.audit/pending_pins.tsv` (T042a) |
| CPA run record | what a task cites for a CPA run, since nothing of the run lands in the tracked tree: after the run has exited, the caller captures `$CPA_RUN/report.json` and `$CPA_RUN/verify.json` with `tools/evidence/evrec -- cat <file>` (an `ev/1` entry whose stdout blob `$EV/blobs/<sha256>` is the file byte for byte, so its sha256 is the file's), or, before the recorder exists (T050), copies them into the task's `$EV/<wp>/` folder with their lines in `$EV/<wp>/SHA256SUMS`; the entry, blob or copy is an ordinary member of the caller's next declared change set, committed by a later CPA run; git itself records every run that committed (its `CPA-Run:` trailer) |
| clean tracked tree | what every clean-tree gate measures (the T161 freeze, the `dirty` field of the T225 and T300 run manifests, T085, T178, T302 and the WP-73 final checks): `scripts/repo/verify_repos.sh --no-remote` (T032) reports `summary.dirty` equal to `summary.dirty_excepted`, each repository judged by `git status --porcelain --ignore-submodules=all` with untracked files counted and ignored paths such as `.audit/` outside it, excepted rows coming only from the reviewed `scripts/repo/exceptions.tsv` (T032); pointer drift is reported beside it, a drift equal to a row of `.audit/pending_pins.tsv` named as a pending pin move (T042a); because CPA writes nothing into the tracked tree, the tree is clean right after a CPA run that exits 0 or 14, and the gate cites the CPA run record of the latest run by its report sha256 |
| `$AUD` | `$FEAT/audit`; one `finding/1` file per finding at `$AUD/findings/<FND-NNNN>.json` and the derived per-run index `$AUD/runs/<run>/findings.index.jsonl` (P3 entry block); no audit output goes under a folder named `out/` (`.gitignore:112`) |
| `$WI` / `$DB` | the constitution `workable-items` binary and `docs/workable_items.db` (ODG-11 default, OD-11) |
| `$FEAT/contracts/` | the JSON schemas of this feature; every schema path is written with the `$FEAT/` prefix because containers run with `--workdir /src` (the repository root), where a bare `contracts/` path does not exist |
| `locked.sh` | `scripts/register/locked.sh -- <cmd>` (T064): a host control-plane launcher that takes `flock docs/.register.lock` (docs/04 §12.2 single writer) and runs exactly one mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command against `$DB` inside IMG-TESTUTIL through `RUNP --rw docs`, so the image's `sqlite3` and the engine binary write the register and a host `sqlite3` (the only one on `PATH` at plan review was an Android SDK copy) never does; binary versions and sha256 in `$EV/wp06/register-binaries.json`; every mutating register command from T069 on goes through it, and a bare mutating call is a review finding |
| ratchet baseline | `scripts/repo/validate_baselines/<check>.tsv` (T040): one row per `(check, path, rule)` with its measured count, for the repository-wide checks that CPA stage S3 runs, each registered in `scripts/repo/validate_checks.tsv` (the anti-bluff scan and the revision-header check, T040; `check_pins.sh`, T105; the QA bank validator, T210); S3 fails when a count rises, when a new key appears, or when a count falls below its row while the change set leaves the row unchanged, so a baseline may only shrink and follows every reduction (§11.4.135). Bootstrap rule: a baseline file is created only in the change set that registers its check, with a header naming the commit it was measured on and the review verdict that covers it (a verdict file that exists with GO when the file is committed); after that, no row is added and no count raised |
| deliberate-violation fixture | a test fixture that violates a repository-wide check on purpose (a seeded bluff, an unpinned image, a fake secret, a placeholder bank step): by default materialised by its test in a temporary directory, never committed; otherwise committed only under a root listed in the reviewed data file `scripts/repo/fixture_roots.txt` (T040a; each row a root, the checks it is exempt from and the reason, added in the change that creates the root), which those checks read as their only exclusion; the same violation outside a listed root still fires (golden-false fixture of T040a) |
| `RUNP <IMG-ID> -- cmd` | base layer: `scripts/containers/run_pinned.sh <IMG-ID> -- cmd` (WP-09); takes an image id, resolves it to `reference@sha256:<digest>` from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml` and composes one rootless `podman run` with the limits, after the disk-headroom precondition `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh` (T001); `RUNP --rw docs` adds the one writable source bind it accepts, `docs/` at `/src/docs` over the read-only source mount, used only by `locked.sh` (T003, T007); `RUNP --out <dir>` binds `<dir>` at `/out`, the only other writable mount (default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`, T003) |
| `runner(IMG-x)` | middle layer: the WP-12 per-toolchain wrapper for image IMG-x (`scripts/containers/run_go.sh`, `run_node.sh`, `run_docs.sh`, `run_scan.sh`, `run_playwright.sh`, `run_testutil.sh` for IMG-TESTUTIL, T120, and `run_qa.sh` for IMG-QA, T212), built on top of `RUNP` and adding the envelope limits, long-op registration and the anti-mess sweep |
| `TIC <app> <lane> -- cmd` | top layer: the WP-12 lane dispatcher `scripts/test-in-container.sh <app> <lane> -- cmd` (T121); takes an application and a lane (not an image id), looks the row up in `scripts/containers/lanes.tsv` and dispatches through the matching `run_*.sh` wrapper (the `tooling` rows to `run_testutil.sh`, IMG-TESTUTIL; the `docs` rows to `run_docs.sh`, IMG-DOCS; the `qa` rows, added by T212, to `run_qa.sh`), which in turn calls `RUNP`-style `podman run`. The three names are layered, not one mechanism: before WP-12 only `RUNP` exists |
| `CPA` | `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (WP-04, docs/21 IC-16): every commit and push from its recorded adoption (T047, after the T046 G-GATE GO) goes through it, in commit windows (P0-P1 conventions), on `main`, fast-forward only, never `--force`; the declared change set is the explicit path list `--paths-from FILE`, where a path with a review verdict in its second column is committed as a held commit, pushed only once that verdict is GO (`--awaits-review <verdict>` holds every path of the run); `--local-only` commits without pushing, `--repo <path>` commits inside one owned repository (T042a); CPA never writes into the tracked tree and never commits its own outputs, which stay in `$CPA_RUN`; each commit it creates carries the trailer `CPA-Run: <run_id>` and, in its message body, the run's deferral flags (`Deferred-Gates:` with `SKIP_LONG`, `SWEEP_ABSENT` or `LOCAL_ONLY`), on a held commit the review it waits for (`Awaits-Review: <verdict path>`), and the commits S1 brought in from elsewhere (`Foreign-Commit: <sha>`) (T042) |
| `ST-xx` | work stream of docs/21 section 4.1 |
| `HC-n` | human checkpoint of docs/21 section 3.3; every record is written to `$EV/hc/HC-<n>[-<USn>].json` |
| `OD-nn` (open, ungrouped) | one of the 15 open `research.md` decisions of docs/21 section 8.6 that no ODG group covers; imported as its own `Operator-blocked` item (T070, T071) and named `BLOCKED-ON OD-nn` in the tasks of the work package it blocks, with the documented partial-proceed behaviour where one exists |

The per-phase-group convention blocks below add detail; where one of them names an abbreviation differently from this table, this table governs and the difference is a plan-review finding.

---

## Phase P0 Foundation and Phase P1 Containerized infrastructure

**Source**: docs/21 §3.3 (P0, P1), §4, §5.1, §5.2, §8, §10; detailed steps in docs/02, 04, 06, 11, 12, 15, 16, 19, 20.

**Phase-group conventions (P0-P1)**

- `$FEAT` = `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation`; `$EV` = `$FEAT/evidence` (ledger, anchors, blobs, verdicts, docs/06 §11); `$AUD` = `$FEAT/audit`.
- `RUNP <IMG-ID> -- <cmd>` = `scripts/containers/run_pinned.sh <IMG-ID> -- <cmd>` (delivered by T007): resolves `<IMG-ID>` to `reference@sha256:<digest>` from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml`, runs rootless podman with `--userns=keep-id --cap-drop=ALL --memory --memory-swap --cpus --pids-limit --label project=catalogizer --label op_id=<id>`, `-v "$PWD":/src:ro -v <out>:/out:rw` (`<out>` from `--out <dir>`, default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`); refuses a tag-only or unresolved image; `--rw docs` adds `-v "$PWD/docs":/src/docs:rw`, the only writable source bind it accepts, for the register writes of `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064).
- Every executable artifact is test-first (§11.4.224): the test task precedes the implementation task and the RED run is captured before code exists; every gate ships a paired §1.1 mutation that is observed failing before the gate is trusted (G-GATE).
- Until `tools/evidence/evrec` exists (WP-05), evidence is a transcript under `$EV/<wp>/` plus `$EV/<wp>/SHA256SUMS`; T095 (P0 exit gate) re-records every P0 transcript as an `ev/1` ledger entry (`$FEAT/contracts/evidence-record.schema.json`).
- Image-to-test binding: IMG-TESTUTIL (bash, git, sqlite3, python3 with `jsonschema`, `PyYAML` and `pytest`, jq) runs every P0 test that needs those tools; IMG-KCOV is kcov built on the IMG-TESTUTIL base (T006), so a coverage run also has git, jq and python3; IMG-SHELLCHECK only lints. T008 checks every (test, image) pair, not a union over images.
- Commit path: before CPA adoption is recorded (T047, after the T046 G-GATE GO), commits are plain fast-forward commits on `main` (never `--force`, never a rewrite); from T047 on every commit goes through `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (docs/21 IC-16) in commit windows (below). No commit lands on `main` before HC-0 (T014): docs/21 §3.3 makes plan acceptance at HC-0 the P0 entry; this list lets the read-only, preparation and test-first tasks start before T014 so that the owner request list (T013) exists to present at HC-0 (a recorded deviation), and every committing step of P0 depends on T014.
- Read-only `git`, CodeGraph CLI reads, Lumen read-only calls, the read-only verifier and the control-plane `podman run` launcher run on the host (docs/16 §5, §18); everything that compiles, tests, lints, scans or renders runs in a pinned rootless container.
- `[REVIEW]` tasks are owned by stream ST-REV (Opus, xhigh, §11.4.209), never by the producing stream, iterated to zero blocking findings (§11.4.134), verdict files under `$EV/reviews/`.
- A path a P0-P1 task creates that does not exist at the plan commit is new and a deliverable of the task that names it, whether or not the text marks it `(new)` (same convention as the P4-P5 block).
- `BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn` means the step waits for the owner (docs/21 §8); a documented research.md default is named where one exists; a missing device, host, credential or service is recorded `blocked-unavailable` with its reason, never a pass (FR-025). `BLOCKED-ON OD-nn` does the same for the 15 open ungrouped decisions of docs/21 §8.6.
- ODG-34 (quota and budget) applies from P0 onward as a reversible working default under the docs/21 planning-rule-6 exception (docs/21 §8, "ODG-34 and ODG-35 in use"): at most 6 working agents, Sonnet as the default working model, Opus at xhigh for every review, the doc02 §13 budget rules. No `research.md` default backs it; it stays an `Operator-blocked` item the owner may overturn, and overturning it changes scheduling only, never an evidence rule.
- Disk headroom (§12.9; measured at plan review: root filesystem 96% used with 86 GB free, podman storage 76 GB): every `podman build`, `podman pull` and container run (RUNP, and the tasks that build or pull images: T006, T106-T113, T114, T143, T144, T149, T151, T211, T270) first runs `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh --op <op_id>` (T001), which reads the free bytes of the podman graphroot (`podman info --format '{{.Store.GraphRoot}}'`) and of the repository filesystem, refuses below the absolute byte threshold `min_free_bytes` in `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.conf` (never a percentage; derived from measured image sizes, T001) with reason `disk_below_headroom`, and records the before and after values in `$EV/disk/<op_id>.json`; every image this list builds carries `--label project=catalogizer`, and cleanup removes only images and containers with that label (`podman image prune --filter label=project=catalogizer`), never a global prune (§11.4.122).
- Tracked deviations until the owner answers (registered by T092, listed in the P0 and P1 exit records, never reported compliant): (a) §11.4.173 places image production on the remote build host; until ODG-07 is answered (its options include 'local containers only', presented in T013), images are built and containers run on the local host in rootless podman; (b) §11.4.76 and §11.4.161 make the containers submodule the sole orchestration layer; RUNP and the runners compose `podman run` directly unless T002 finds a run primitive in `submodules/containers` to wrap, and the missing primitive is proposed upstream with T139 (§11.4.74).
- Single writer (docs/04 §12.2): from T069 on, every mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command against `$DB` runs through `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064), which runs it in IMG-TESTUTIL through `RUNP --rw docs`; read-only queries use the `immutable=1` URI (T071).
- Repository-wide checks that fail on the current tree (the anti-bluff scan, revision headers, `check_pins.sh`, the QA bank validator) run as ratchets against their measured baselines under the bootstrap rule of the abbreviation table, never as whole-repository pass/fail stages that would refuse every commit; fixtures that violate such a check on purpose follow the deliberate-violation fixture convention of the same table (T040a).
- Every task that writes a `docs/scripts/*.md` companion adds its row to the index `docs/scripts/README.md` in the same change; T036 creates the index and lists the companions that exist when it runs.
- Commit windows (from T047 on; §11.4.84): the working tree is shared by every stream, and CPA refuses an uncommitted file that its declared change set does not name (13 at S7, 20 at the S0 sweep once T093 wires it), so commits are batched: at a window every stream stops writing, releases its long-op registrations and hands the conductor (ST-GOV) all of its uncommitted paths, each with the review verdict it still waits for, if any; the conductor runs CPA with their union as `--paths-from` (the stream of each path named in the commit message): the paths with no pending review form the first commit and are pushed, the paths still waiting for a review are committed after them as held commits (`Awaits-Review:`, T042) whose push waits for that verdict to be GO; after every window the tracked tree is clean and no unreviewed change has reached a remote; a held commit also holds back the push of every later commit until its GO (S6, T042), so reviews of held work are scheduled first; a task that commits only after its review GO (for example T029) keeps that guarantee for the remotes: at an earlier window its in-flight paths become commits held on that review, and its commit step adds the rest together with the verdict; a clean-tree gate (T161, T225) runs right after a window.
- Container outputs: a containerized step writes only to `/out` (RUNP `--out <dir>`, default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`, T003); a host step copies each file the task keeps to its tracked path (under `$EV`, `$AUD` or the source tree) and records the source and destination sha256 in its `ev/1` entry, or in its `SHA256SUMS` line before the recorder exists; no container writes `/src`, except `scripts/register/locked.sh` through `RUNP --rw docs` (T064).
- New Markdown files: every tracked `*.md` file a task creates (evidence notes and `docs/scripts/*.md` companions included) carries the §11.4.44 revision header table (rows `Revision`, `Created`, `Last modified`, `Status`) in its first 40 lines, so the S3 revision-header ratchet (T040) meets no new key.

### WP-09 Bootstrap pinned-image slice for the P0 tests (stream ST-INFRA, size M, FR-021, SC-012)

No inputs (local rootless podman only; no remote host, no owner decision). Starts together with WP-02 and WP-03; WP-04, WP-05, WP-06 and every P0 test leg depend on it (docs/21 IC-35).

- [ ] T001 [P] [US3] [TDD] Write the failing test `scripts/containers/tests/test_probe_host.sh` then implement `scripts/containers/probe_host.sh` (local part of docs/16 §8.5: `/dev/kvm`, SELinux, `ulimit -u`, cgroup version, rootless flag, `user.slice` `memory.max`, `nproc`, `MemTotal`/`MemAvailable`) writing `$EV/host-probe.json`; in the same task, test-first `scripts/containers/tests/test_disk_headroom.sh` then `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh` and its threshold file `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.conf` (the §12.9 free-space minimum as consumer DATA; no numeric value for it was found in `Constitution.md` at plan time): the threshold is an absolute byte count `min_free_bytes`, never a percentage (measured at plan review: root filesystem 96% used with about 85 GB free, so any percentage floor above 4% would refuse every build), derived as twice the largest measured size of an image this list uses plus the largest measured build scratch peak, with the formula, its inputs and their measurement dates written in the file (first derived here from `podman image inspect --format '{{.Size}}'` of the images already in local storage or, when none is present, from the layer sizes the registry manifests of the four WP-09 base images report, read without pulling, the factor two being the stated margin for their compression; re-derived by T006 once the WP-09 images exist and by T115 once `build/containers/profile.json` holds the measured peaks), reviewed in T010; a caller also passes `--need <bytes>`, the size of the image it pulls or builds (from the lock entry, or from the registry manifest before a first pull); with `podman info` and `df` shims a graphroot or repository filesystem whose free bytes minus the need fall below the threshold is refused with reason `disk_below_headroom` and a non-zero exit, a value above it passes, a percentage value in the conf file is refused as a configuration error, before and after values go to `$EV/disk/<op_id>.json` or, when `DISK_HEADROOM_OUT_DIR` is set, to that directory (CPA sets it to `$CPA_RUN/disk/`, T042), and an unreadable free-space value is refused, never treated as enough; RED: the test run before `probe_host.sh` exists exits non-zero and is captured to `$EV/wp09/probe-red.txt`; a control needle reads a path that must not exist and records the expected absence; an errored probe is `error`, never `absent`; a host without SELinux (no `/sys/fs/selinux`, no `getenforce`) is recorded `absent`, never `error`; `mkdir -p $EV/wp09` precedes the RED run (host shell is allowed here, docs/21 §3.3 P0)

## Phase P2: Inventory and baselines (doc21 §3.3 P2, §5.3)

**Entry**: WP-06 register operational, WP-05 recorder available, WP-13 stack available for baseline test runs.
**Exit (doc21 §3.3)**: `SELECT count(*) FROM v_unmapped_entries` returns 0 and the doc04 §13.1 per-source totals show `entries = mapped` for every `reg_sources` row (docs/21 IC-39: the docs/03 staging query on `src_entry ... disposition='PENDING'` names a table the docs/04 v4 DDL does not have; there the entries live in `reg_source_entries` and the 'no PENDING entry' rule is this view); every source row mapped with its `reg_source_map.relation` in the closed set; every docs/21 §9.1 seed and every §9.5 entry a mapped source entry; every external tracker recorded `queried` or `skipped(reason)`; `applicability.yaml` with no `?` cell; coverage baseline per application recorded. **Human checkpoint HC-2** (T222, `$EV/hc/HC-2.json`): SC-001 reconciliation reviewed; ODG-09 legacy-closure policy and doc04 D-6 category mapping confirmed; the reviewed exit record `$EV/p2-exit.json` (T220, T221) precedes it.

**Phase-group conventions (P2-P3)** (abbreviations only, no new mechanism):
- `$EV` = `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation/evidence/` (doc06 §11 layout); every PASS/FAIL below is an `ev/1` entry in `$EV/ledger.jsonl` written by the recorder `tools/evidence/evrec` (WP-05), validated against `$FEAT/contracts/evidence-record.schema.json`.
- `$WI` = `submodules/constitution/scripts/workable-items/bin/workable-items`; `$DB` = `docs/workable_items.db` (ODG-11 default, OD-11). Items are created only through the pre-minted `reg_ids` path of doc04 §12.3 step 1, and every mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command runs through `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064), including the minting of `FND-NNNN` ids by the P3 agents, of which up to six run at once.
- "runner(IMG-x)" = the WP-12 container runner wrapper for image IMG-x (rootless podman, digest from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml`, `--memory` and `--pids-limit` set, `jobs=1` until per-job memory is measured, doc16 §8.2). Its exact script path is fixed by WP-12: `scripts/containers/run_<toolchain>.sh` (T120) and the lane dispatcher `scripts/test-in-container.sh <app> <lane> -- <cmd>` (TIC, T121); no task here invents another. Git, index reads and the read-only verifier run on the host (doc16 §18); nothing builds or tests on the bare host (§11.4.173).
- Gate scripts: `scripts/register/gate.sh` (doc04 §12.3 step 2; `--completion` mode for step 3, T180); exports through `scripts/register/export.sh` and `reconcile.sh` (T067). The disk-headroom precondition, the tracked deviations, the ratchet rule, the commit windows, the container-output rule and the new-Markdown header rule of the P0-P1 conventions apply here unchanged. Commits only through `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (WP-04, IC-16) in commit windows, on `main`, fast-forward to every upstream, never force; with up to six P3 agents writing at once, each stream's uncommitted files wait for the next window, and a clean-tree gate (T161, T225) runs right after one.
- File names marked "(new)" are proposed here where the source document names the tool but not its path; the reviewer of the WP confirms the path. Every other path a P2-P3 task creates that does not exist at the plan commit is likewise new and a deliverable of the task that names it, mark or no mark.
- `[REVIEW]` tasks are owned by stream ST-REV on Opus at xhigh (§11.4.209), never by the producing stream (§11.4.240).

## Phase P3: Audit pass (doc21 §3.3 P3, §5.4)

**Entry**: P1 detectors pinned with needles (WP-15); index gate PASS in the current pass; register accepting findings. **Parallelism**: up to six read-only audit agents, one per unit (doc02 §14.3 waves W1-W3); heavy detectors queued at K = min(floor(18 GiB / measured peak RSS), 4); one Android container at a time; `ulimit -u` headroom checked before each wave (§12.12). **Exit**: every unit has a recorded audit result; second run from an identical manifest yields `IDENTICAL` (SC-002); every finding carries location, severity, category, evidence and register link (FR-007). **HC-3**: owner accepts the audit report. Every detector has golden-good, golden-bad and a control needle before any zero result is trusted (doc06 §10, §11.4.201(7)(b)). Each finding is a `finding/1` record (`$FEAT/contracts/finding.schema.json`) with an `FND-NNNN` id minted from `reg_ids` through `scripts/register/locked.sh`, written once as `$AUD/findings/<FND-NNNN>.json` (the finding store of doc02 §9 and data-model); a later run that finds the same `fingerprint` appends its run id to that file's `run_ids` and mints nothing (§11.4.214). The per-run `$AUD/runs/<run-id>/findings.index.jsonl` is a derived projection (doc02 §9): one line per finding with `unit`, `path`, `line_start`, `rule_id` and `fingerprint`, sorted by `(unit, path, line_start, rule_id)`, with no timestamps, no `run_ids` and no minted id, so two runs on identical input give byte-identical index files (SC-002). The disk-headroom precondition and the single-writer rule of the P0-P1 conventions apply to every P3 container run and register write.

## Phase P4: Contract layer (docs/21 §3.3 P4, §5.5)

**Entry**: WP-30 backend audit accepted at G-AUDIT (WP-39R GO); WP-13 real-service stack running; WP-05 evidence recorder available.
**Exit**: contract inventory CT-1..CT-22 (docs/01 §14) each with a test on both sides or a recorded n/a; every client drift mismatch registered as a finding; HC-4 held for compatibility-window changes.

**Phase-group conventions (P4-P5)**:

- **Paths**: `$SPEC` = `$FEAT` = `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation`; `$EV` = `$SPEC/evidence`; `$AUD` = `$SPEC/audit`. Every `[REVIEW]` verdict is written to `$EV/reviews/<WP>[-<item>].json` (one folder for the whole task list); human-checkpoint records go to `$EV/hc/HC-<n>[-<USn>].json`. A path marked `(new)` does not exist yet and is a deliverable of the task that names it (or of the task named in the mark); generated evidence files under `$EV/` and `$AUD/` are all new and are not marked one by one, and paths created by P0-P3 tasks (for example `scripts/commit-push-all.sh`, `tools/evidence/*`, `scripts/build/verify_artifact.sh`, `scripts/register/gate.sh`, `scripts/qa/run_profile.sh`, `$SPEC/matrix/applicability.yaml`, `docs/DOC_SCOPE.yaml`) are not marked here. A new path under a directory that `.gitignore` ignores today (`tools/`, `build/`, `coverage/`) depends on the WP-09 `.gitignore` negation task of P0; paths that no negation covers (`reports/`, `qa-results/`, any `coverage/` directory below the root) are not used here. Top-level `$EV` entries first written in P4-P7 (all listed in the docs/06 §11 layout), each written only by the tasks named: `exceptions/` (T440; T582 only reads it), `flake_ledger.jsonl` (T563), `p5-exit.json` (T457), `p6-exit.json` (T557), `pack/` (T588, T589), `qa/` (T462, T463, T474; also P2 WP-24), `register/` (T576; also P2 WP-20), `release_digests/` (T448), `reproducibility/` (T443), `retest/` (T567, T570), `sbom/` (T445), `verify/` (T581, T582). No `$EV` or `$AUD` path is written by the commit-push script (next bullets).
- **Identifiers**: `CT-n` is contract n of the docs/01 §14 inventory (written `Cn` there); `BC-n` is backend candidate n of the docs/07 candidate table (also written `Cn` there). The doc12 conversion classes C1..C6 keep their doc12 names.
- **Containerized runner (TIC)**: `scripts/test-in-container.sh <app> <lane> -- <cmd>` (created in WP-12 (T121), a dispatcher over the T120 wrappers `scripts/containers/run_go.sh`, `run_node.sh`, `run_docs.sh`, `run_scan.sh`, `run_playwright.sh`, plus `scripts/containers/run_qa.sh` for the rows of the `qa` app, which run in IMG-QA, the WP-24 QA image of T211 (helixqa, Tesseract, ffmpeg, Playwright browsers); `run_qa.sh` and the `qa` rows of `scripts/containers/lanes.tsv` are delivered by T212, on which every `TIC qa` call depends, named in each such task together with T211; lanes `unit`, `contract`, `integration`, `e2e`, `api`, `docs`, `tooling`; digest-pinned images from `build/containers/images.lock.yaml`). The rows of the `tooling` app (`TIC tooling <lane>`, called by T309 to T312, T319, T324, T420, T453, T510, T514, T553, T570 and T584) run in IMG-TESTUTIL through `run_testutil.sh`, the wrapper the WP-12 lane table names for that app key (abbreviation table); that image must carry python3, pytest, PyYAML, jsonschema, bash, git and jq, proven by a smoke run of `python3 -c 'import pytest, yaml, jsonschema'` and `jq --version` inside it, and a `TIC tooling` RED counts only after that smoke has passed in the same image (a RED caused by a missing interpreter or module is a RED for the wrong reason, §11.4.224(A)); the WP-60 converter tests use `TIC qa tooling` in IMG-QA instead (T460). The rows of the `docs` app (`TIC docs docs`, T520, T542, T546 and T547; the Python validator of T533 runs in the same image) run in IMG-DOCS through `run_docs.sh`, which therefore needs python3 and pytest besides its render tools (docs/16 §6.1 lists them in the IMG-DOCS row), under the same smoke-before-RED rule. Every build of a deliverable artifact (Go binary, web image, Rust bundle, Android APK) runs in its build container on the remote build host through the WP-14 mechanism (`build/hosts.env`, `build/components.json`, `cmd/distributed-build`) and returns the artifact with a sha256 manifest checked by `scripts/build/verify_artifact.sh`; when the host is unavailable the build is BLOCKED-ON ODG-07 (input, cannot be defaulted), never moved to the bare host. No build or test runs on the bare host (§11.4.173, FR-021).
- **Evidence**: every run goes through `tools/evidence/evrec` into `$EV/ledger.jsonl` as an `ev/1` record (`$SPEC/contracts/evidence-record.schema.json`); item verdicts are derived by `tools/evidence/verdict` into `$EV/verdicts/<ATM-id>.json`, never written by hand (review verdicts never go there).
- **Commit-push outputs and clean-tree checks** (abbreviation table: `$CPA_RUN`, CPA run record, clean tracked tree): the commit-push script (CPA, WP-04, T042) writes nothing into the tracked tree and commits none of its own outputs: its `report.json`, `summary.txt`, S7 verifier JSON `verify.json`, stage transcripts and deferral rows stay in its ignored run directory `$CPA_RUN` = `.audit/commit-push/<run_id>/` (never tmpfs; `<run_id>` = UTC time, process id and a random suffix), and a run that does not get the lock writes only its own run directory and touches nothing else. Git itself is the tracked record of a push: every commit CPA makes carries the trailer `CPA-Run: <run_id>` and, in its message body, its deferral flags (`Deferred-Gates:` with `SKIP_LONG`, `SWEEP_ABSENT` or `LOCAL_ONLY`) and, on a held commit, `Awaits-Review: <verdict path>`. A task that needs a run's report or verifier result as evidence cites the CPA run record: after the run it captures `$CPA_RUN/report.json` (and `verify.json` where needed) with `tools/evidence/evrec`, an `ev/1` entry whose blob `$EV/blobs/<sha256>` is the file byte for byte, and that entry and blob are ordinary members of the task's next declared change set. Shared stores (`$EV/ledger.jsonl`, `$EV/blobs/`) belong to the change set of whichever task wrote to them; a CPA run refuses only untracked or modified files outside its declared change set (T042). Every clean-tree or `dirty` condition of P4-P7 (T429, T442, T582, T595b) is measured on the clean tracked tree, where the ignored `.audit/` directories never count, and cites the CPA run record of the latest run by its report sha256.
- **Generated tracked files**: the container-outputs rule of the P0-P1 conventions applies (a containerized step writes only to `/out`, RUNP `--out <dir>`, default the ignored `.audit/out/<op_id>/`; no container writes `/src` except `scripts/register/locked.sh` through `RUNP --rw docs`, T064). In P4-P7 every tracked file a containerized step generates or edits (a route baseline or dump, a drift or contract report, a converted bank file, a regenerated bank-id floor, a wave assignment, a dependency report, a schema dump, an index, a diagram render, an export twin, a badge or a generated reference; for example T312, T314, T319, T453, T454, T462, T463 to T472, T523, T534 to T537, T541, T542 and T545) is written under `/out` at its repository-relative path, and a host step of the same task, after the container has exited, copies each such file to that path and records the `/out` source and the tracked destination with their sha256, which must be equal, in the task's `ev/1` entry; a test that asserts a generated file asserts it under `/out` (or under the output variable that points there). The copy is a file operation, not a build or a test, so it does not breach the no-bare-host rule.
- **Commit windows (parallel streams)**: the P0-P1 commit-window rule applies (§11.4.84): the working tree is shared by every stream and CPA refuses an uncommitted file its declared change set does not name, so at a window every stream stops writing, releases its long-op registrations and hands the conductor (ST-GOV) its uncommitted paths, each with the review verdict it still waits for, and the conductor runs one CPA run over their union as `--paths-from` (T042); a stream never declares another stream's files to get past a refusal, and a `--repo <path>` run, whose S0 sweep is scoped to `<path>` (T042a), is not stopped by another stream's uncommitted files elsewhere.
- **Fix protocol (FP)** for every [TDD] fix task in P5 (docs/06 §4, §11.4.115(F)): (1) root-cause note on the register item (`docs/workable_items.db`, FR-008, §11.4.102); (2) RED `ev/1` entry on the pre-fix artifact fingerprint, observed before the fix is written; (3) the fix; (4) GREEN on a different artifact fingerprint, three identical GREEN iterations; (5) reviewer-authored mutation recorded as a caught `MUTATION` entry (§11.4.194(6)(d)); (6) clean-target verification of the built artifact (FR-021, §11.4.200); (7) commit through `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (CPA) on `main` only, no force-push (FR-024, §11.4.113). Work committed inside a submodule repository (T430, T431, T433, T499, T501, T572 where the strengthened test lives in a submodule, T581) uses the per-repository mode `CPA --repo <path>` of WP-04 (T042a): it commits in, and pushes from, that repository only, to that repository's remotes as enumerated at run time, and leaves every ancestor repository's HEAD and gitlink unchanged; a gitlink moves only through a pointer commit in the parent that has passed its G-PIN review (docs/11 §6.4 step 7: T441 before the T442 layer commits of WP-55, T580a before the final pointer moves of WP-73 T581), never as a side effect of a module commit. Each `--repo` commit records its pending pin move (repository path, new gitlink sha, run id) in the ignored file `.audit/pending_pins.tsv`; a main-repository CPA run reports a pin drift that exactly matches a pending row as pending, not failing, while any other drift still exits 15; the pointer commit of the G-PIN layer (T442, T581) consumes, that is removes, the rows of the pins it moves; and the S0 sweep of a `--repo` run is scoped to `<path>` (T042a). The same pending rows are what the standalone verifier runs of WP-55 accept as pending (T429, T435, T442).
- **blocked-unavailable**: a test needing an absent real service, credential or device records `verdict: blocked` with `blocked_reason` and `counts_as: not_passing` (FR-025, docs/05 §10); it is never a pass or a skip.
- **Producer is not verifier**: every [REVIEW] task is a pure review owned by ST-REV on Opus at xhigh (§11.4.209, §11.4.240), iterates to zero blocking findings (§11.4.134), writes its verdict to `$EV/reviews/`, and never produces the reviewed work; fixes found by a review go back to the producing stream.
- **Review before push**: a change made before the `[REVIEW]` GO that covers it is committed as a held commit: its paths go into the declared change set with that review's verdict file in the second column of the `--paths-from` list (or the whole run is held with `--awaits-review <verdict file>`), so the commit carries `Deferred-Gates: LOCAL_ONLY` and `Awaits-Review: <verdict file>` beside its `CPA-Run` trailer (WP-04 T039, T042; `--repo <path>` runs hold the same way, T042a). S6 of every later CPA run, main-repository and `--repo` alike, pushes nothing past a held commit until a change set has committed its verdict file with `verdict: GO`; until then the run exits 14 with reason `review_pending` naming each held commit and its verdict. So no stream's run publishes another stream's unreviewed commit on the shared `main`, and no task pushes unreviewed work to any remote (§11.4.142, §11.4.125); the owed push is made by the first CPA run whose change set adds that GO, or by a later one, and is not a dependency edge of the producing task. The awaited verdict file must not already hold GO when the held commit is made: a commit made after a GO waits on the verdict file of a new review iteration (`<WP>[-<item>]-r<n>.json`), so an earlier GO never covers later work; and every verdict lists the commits it reviewed in a `covers_runs` field (repository path, commit sha and `CPA-Run` id, read from the commit trailers). Review verdict files and human-checkpoint records are gate records, committed and pushed by ordinary runs. In P5 the commit of FP step 7 is pushed only after the review task of its work package returns GO for that change (T359, T360, T378, T379, T395, T398, T411, T428, T432, T434a, T441, T451, T456); in P4 the G-CONTRACT (T322) and WP-41 (T336) reviews gate the pushes.
- **Register writes**: every mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command on `docs/workable_items.db` runs through `scripts/register/locked.sh -- <cmd>` (T064), which holds `flock docs/.register.lock` around exactly one command (single writer, doc04 §12.2; a bare mutating call is a review finding), in IMG-TESTUTIL (the image T008 maps to `sqlite3`); read-only queries use the `immutable=1` URI (T071); a bulk write adds the hardlinked `cp -al` pre-op backup, `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`, `scripts/register/gate.sh` and the T066 dump-and-commit procedure (DB, `docs/register/register.sql` and the documents regenerated by `scripts/register/export.sh` and `scripts/register/reconcile.sh` of T067, committed together). Findings are written as one `finding/1` file per finding under `$AUD/findings/` and linked to their register item through ST-REG.
- **Inherited P0-P3 rules** (abbreviation table and P0-P1 conventions, unchanged here): (a) disk headroom: every local `podman build`, `podman pull` and container run of P4-P7 (every `RUNP` and `TIC` call through the precondition built into `RUNP`, and every direct pull of a pinned tool image, for example in T317, T445 and T543) first runs `scripts/containers/disk_headroom.sh --op <op_id>` (T001), is refused with `disk_below_headroom` below the threshold and records its before and after values in `$EV/disk/<op_id>.json`; images built here carry `--label project=catalogizer` and cleanup removes only labelled images and containers, never a global prune; (b) every task that writes a `docs/scripts/*.md` companion (§11.4.18) adds its row to the index `docs/scripts/README.md` (T036) in the same change, and a companion written beside its script (for example `scripts/contract/README.md`, T309) gets an index row pointing to it; (c) the ratchet baselines `scripts/repo/validate_baselines/<check>.tsv` (T040) only shrink: a task that removes counted violations lowers or deletes their rows in the same change (T448 for `check_pins.tsv`, T472 and T474 for `qa_banks.tsv` and the anti-bluff baseline), and no task raises a count.
- **Owner decisions**: `BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn` names one of the 40 docs/21 §8 groups ODG-01..ODG-40 (a `research.md` decision grouped there, such as OD-21 in ODG-17 or OD-32 in ODG-15, is written `BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn (OD-nn)`) and `BLOCKED-ON OD-nn` one of the 15 open ungrouped decisions of docs/21 §8.6; where `research.md` §5 lists a reversible default it is quoted, otherwise the docs/21 recommendation is stated as a proposal only and the dependent step stays blocked.
- **ODG-35 working default (P5)**: under the docs/21 planning-rule-6 exception (docs/21 §8, "ODG-34 and ODG-35 in use") P5 proceeds on ODG-35's recommendation as a reversible working default: no SLA tiers, the FR-008 completion rule replaces them, and fixes are ordered by severity then exposure (docs/15 §12, §13). No `research.md` default backs it; it stays an `Operator-blocked` item the owner may overturn, which changes scheduling only, never an evidence rule. ODG-34 (P0 conventions) continues to apply.

## Phase P5: Remediation (docs/21 §3.3 P5, §5.6)

**Entry**: P4 accepted for the contracts each fix touches; each register item root-caused (FR-008).
**Ordering**: server first, then each client against the fixed contract; within a stream by severity then exposure (docs/15 §13; the ODG-35 working default, no SLA tiers); a contract-changing fix lands with its client changes and both-sided tests in one reviewed change set. Every task follows the Fix protocol (FP) above.
**Exit**: every finding in each stream `Fixed` with custody chain or closed by an allowed basis; blocked items listed with exact reason.

the peeled `^{}` line of an annotated tag gives the tag's commit), a pin equal to a listed tip being `ok` with no fetch; otherwise fetch into the object store only, the remote's default branch first and every other listed head and tag only when the pin is not found there, with `git fetch --no-tags --no-write-fetch-head --refmap= <remote> <ref>` (the empty `--refmap=` stops git from updating the configured remote-tracking ref, so the fetch writes objects only: no working tree, branch ref, remote-tracking ref, `FETCH_HEAD` or gitlink moves; one fetch form with docs/15 §10.3), then decide `git merge-base --is-ancestor <pin> <tip>` per fetched tip; status per remote `ok` (the pin equals or is an ancestor of a tip), `absent` (every tip of a reachable remote read and fetched and none contains the pin) or `unproven` (the remote is unreachable, an `ls-remote` or a fetch failed or timed out, or the repository is a shallow clone, `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` printing `true`, so ancestry cannot be decided; 1 shallow repository at plan time), never assumed present; the row takes the worst status of its remotes (`absent`, then `unproven`, then `ok`) and lists every remote with its own status; because the container source mount is read-only (`RUNP`, T007), this reachability step is a separate script `scripts/deps/pin_reachability.sh` (new), tested with the generator's fixtures through `TIC tooling unit` and run for real on the host as a read-only git step of the control plane like the WP-03 verifier's `--fetch` (docs/16 §5: it writes only objects, never a working tree, branch ref, remote-tracking ref or gitlink), writing `$AUD/deps/pin-reachability.json` (new), which the containerized generator reads; pinned vs latest tip, signed commits (informative), licence (doc15 §10.5 L-1: the SPDX identifier where the root licence file text matches one exactly, `none` where the repository has no licence file, 45 repositories at plan time, 40 own-organisation and 5 third-party; the heuristic class of doc11 §8.5.1 is only a lead; the root-licence entry of the T269 inventory is compared and a disagreement is a finding), `go.mod` `replace` targets, build-copy diffs and `go mod verify` output; a completeness check fails when the submodule row count differs from the recursive count or the root or vendored row is missing; needles: in scratch fixtures with local bare remotes, a planted pin that no remote holds must be reported `absent`, a pin held only behind a tag must be reported `ok`, a pin reachable only from a non-default branch must be reported `ok`, a remote made unreachable must give `unproven` for its rows, never `ok`, a shallow clone must give `unproven`, a row whose remotes disagree must take the worst status, and after every fixture run the remote-tracking refs and `FETCH_HEAD` of the fixture repository are unchanged; run in a pinned image through `TIC tooling unit`; RED before the generator and the reachability script exist; GREEN three runs hash-identical

## Phase P6: Test closure, QA, performance, documentation (WP-60 to WP-65)

**Purpose**: close every applicable test-type cell, convert the HelixQA banks into asserting cases, install the performance gate, and deliver the documentation and definitions programme (docs/21 §3.3 P6).

**Entry**: WP-24 QA wrapper and validator, WP-23 applicability map and baselines, WP-38 performance baselines, WP-37 documentation baseline, WP-40 live route dump, WP-41 contract tests.

**Phase-group conventions (P6-P7)** (all paths repo-relative):

- `$SPEC` = `$FEAT` = `specs/001-full-project-audit-remediation`; `$EV` = `$SPEC/evidence` (doc06 §11 layout: `ledger.jsonl` with `ev/1` entries, `verdicts/<item-id>.json` derived only, `anchors.jsonl`, `matrix/`, `reviews/` for every `[REVIEW]` verdict, `hc/` for human-checkpoint records); `$AUD` = `$SPEC/audit`. The doc06 §11 per-application coverage folders are `$EV/coverage_baseline/<app>/` (the P2 baselines and exclusion-gate records of WP-23, read here as the ratchet floor) and `$EV/coverage_targets/<app>/` (the dated owner targets, ODG-17), both named this way because `.gitignore:139` ignores every directory named `coverage/` at any depth and no WP-09 negation covers it below the root (docs/21 IC-38). `(new)` and `.gitignore` rules as in the P4-P5 conventions.
- `TIC` = the containerized runner `scripts/test-in-container.sh <app> <lane> -- <cmd>` (created in WP-12 (T121) over the T120 wrappers; lanes `unit`, `contract`, `integration`, `e2e`, `api`, `docs`, `tooling`; doc05 §12.5). The rows of the `qa` app (`TIC qa <lane>`) dispatch to `scripts/containers/run_qa.sh` in IMG-QA, the WP-24 QA image of T211 (helixqa, Tesseract, ffmpeg, Playwright browsers); `run_qa.sh` and those rows are delivered by T212, on which every `TIC qa` call below depends (each such task names it together with T211). No test or build runs on the bare host (§11.4.173); every build of a deliverable artifact runs on the remote build host through the WP-14 mechanism (`cmd/distributed-build`, manifest checked by `scripts/build/verify_artifact.sh`, BLOCKED-ON ODG-07 when the host is unavailable), mutation runs in IMG-MUT, docs in IMG-DOCS, QA in IMG-QA.
- `CPA` = `scripts/commit-push-all.sh` (WP-04, IC-16): every commit and push of this task list goes through it, on `main` only, fast-forward only, never `--force` (§11.4.113, FR-020, FR-024); work inside a submodule repository uses its per-repository mode `CPA --repo <path>`, which moves no ancestor HEAD or gitlink and records its pending pin move in `.audit/pending_pins.tsv` (FP step 7 of the P4-P5 block); CPA writes nothing into the tracked tree, its outputs stay in the ignored `.audit/commit-push/<run_id>/`, and a task cites a run report through `evrec` (the P4-P5 commit-push outputs rule).
- Review before push, generated tracked files, commit windows and register writes: the P4-P5 rules apply unchanged (a change made before the `[REVIEW]` GO that covers it is committed as a held commit, `Awaits-Review: <verdict file>`, and pushed only once a change set has committed that verdict file with GO, enforced at S6; a container writes generated tracked files under `/out` and a host step copies them with matching sha256; uncommitted work of parallel streams is committed in the P0-P1 commit windows by the conductor (ST-GOV), and no stream declares another stream's files; every mutating `$WI` or `sqlite3` command on `docs/workable_items.db` runs through `scripts/register/locked.sh` (T064) in IMG-TESTUTIL, bulk writes with the `cp -al` backup and the T066 dump-and-commit procedure; the inherited disk-headroom, `docs/scripts/README.md` row and ratchet-baseline rules of the P4-P5 block apply too).
- "Three-run evidence" = three runs with identical canonical hashes recorded in `$EV/ledger.jsonl` (doc06 §5, SC-003). "Paired mutation" = a mutation observed to turn the test RED, recorded with `mutation: {operator, location, result: "caught"}` (doc05 §8.2).
- `[REVIEW]` tasks are pure reviews owned by stream ST-REV on Opus at xhigh (§11.4.209), never by the producing stream (§11.4.240), iterate to zero blocking findings (§11.4.134), record at least one reviewer-authored mutation where a test or gate is reviewed (§11.4.194(6)(d)), and write their verdict to `$EV/reviews/<WP>[-<item>].json`; any fix goes back to the producing stream.
- A test that needs an absent credential, device, host or service is recorded `blocked-unavailable` with the exact reason (FR-025); it never counts as passing and is never replaced by a mock or a skip.

### WP-60 HelixQA bank conversion waves (stream ST-QA, size XL, FR-009 FR-010 FR-025, SC-004 SC-005)

## Phase P7: Verification and closure (WP-70 to WP-74R)

**Purpose**: final gates on the candidate artifact, register closure, recursive clean-and-pushed state, and the evidence pack with its independent review (docs/21 §3.3 P7).

- [ ] T594 [US3] Final anchor (ST-GOV, the producer of the chain; not a review): after the HC-7 ledger entry of T593 is the last ledger entry, write the final anchor (head hash and entry count) over it to `$EV/anchors.jsonl`, so that truncating any entry after the T585 interim anchor, the T590 to T592 verdict entries and the HC-7 entry included, is detectable (§11.4.268(C)); anchor strength reported `policy` while OD-76 is open (depends on T593).
- [ ] T595 [US3] [REVIEW] ST-REV re-runs the chain verifier against the T594 final anchor, recomputes the head hash and entry count, and checks that a scratch copy of the ledger with its last entry removed is rejected (reviewer-authored truncation needle); its own run is recorded only in its verdict file `$EV/reviews/WP-74R-final-anchor.json` (with the verified head hash and entry count), never appended to the ledger, so the final anchor stays over the last ledger entry; GO (depends on T594).
- [ ] T595a [US7] Closing push (ST-GOV; not a review): one ordinary main-repository CPA run commits every WP-74R output not yet committed (the verdicts of T590, T591, T592 and T595, `$EV/hc/HC-7.json`, the HC-7 ledger entry and the final anchor of T594) and pushes the main repository, fast-forward only, never `--force`, to every remote enumerated at run time; its S6 pushes any still unpushed held commit of WP-73 and WP-74 only because the verdict files those commits name in `Awaits-Review:` (T583, T590) are committed with GO, and a commit it refuses is reported and the push re-run after the missing GO; no `evrec` entry is written for this run, because a ledger entry after the HC-7 entry would sit outside the final anchor of T594: its evidence is its `$CPA_RUN/report.json` (its sha256 checked and reported by T595b, never captured into the ledger) and the commit that carries its `CPA-Run` trailer (compared with every remote tip by T595b) (depends on T595).
- [ ] T595b [US7] Final state check (ST-REV, read-only; not a gate review, so it writes no verdict file): after T595a, ST-REV runs `scripts/repo/verify_repos.sh --strict --json .audit/verify/<run_id>/final-sealed.json` on the tracked tree and checks A-1 to A-7 as in T582 (exit 0, `summary.failing=0`, `summary.unproven=0`, no pending pin move, root tip equal on every remote and equal to the commit that carries the T595a `CPA-Run` trailer), and checks the sha256 of the T595a run report; nothing is written to the tracked tree or to the ledger, so the state this run certifies is the state it measured and the final anchor of T594 stays over the HC-7 entry; the verdict, the sha256 of the verifier JSON and the sha256 of the T595a run report are reported to the owner as the closing statement of the feature, and a failure reopens WP-73 and keeps the feature incomplete (FR-019, SC-010) (depends on T595a).

**Checkpoint WP-74R**: reviewer GO on Opus at xhigh; reviewer-authored mutations detected; zero blocking findings; HC-7 decision recorded; final anchor over the HC-7 entry written (T594) and re-verified by ST-REV with the truncation needle rejected (T595); every WP-73, WP-74 and WP-74R output committed and pushed, each local commit only under the committed GO of the verdict file it awaits (T595a); the strict verifier exit 0 on the tracked tree after the closing push, its JSON sha256 reported to the owner (T595b).

## Dependencies and execution order

- Phase order is strict at the exit gates: **P0 -> P1 -> P2 -> P3 -> P4 -> P5 -> P6 -> P7**. The gate evidence for each phase is the `Checkpoint` line of its last work package plus the human checkpoints HC-1..HC-n in docs/21 section 3.3.
- Inside a phase, work packages follow the Inputs column of docs/21 section 5 (diagram in docs/21 section 5.9, acyclic, checked by script). Tasks marked `[P]` inside one work package, and work packages with disjoint Inputs, may run concurrently, bounded by the host limits in docs/21 section 4.2 (60 percent memory ceiling, process-limit headroom, at most 6 concurrent agents).
- Producer and reviewer streams are separate: `[REVIEW]` tasks belong to ST-REV and never to the stream that produced the work (WP-39R, WP-74R).
- Owner-blocked tasks (`BLOCKED-ON ODG-nn`) are skipped, not guessed; the autonomous loop continues with every unblocked task and records the block (docs/21 section 8).

- **Forward references**: 8 tasks name a later task as an explicit dependency (T027 after T069, T052 after T069, T054 after T069, T042 after T089, T182 after T217, T275 after T279, T469 after T493, T534 after T542). Such a task is scheduled after its dependency regardless of its position in this document; the explicit "depends on" graph over all tasks was checked by script and is acyclic (loose phrases such as "after the read-only source mount" or "for example T011" are narrative, not dependencies).

## Implementation strategy

1. **MVP / first deliverable**: P0 and P1 (verifier, evidence recorder, register, pinned containers), then P2 baselines. After P2 exit the register contains every known item and the baselines exist, which already satisfies US1 on its own.
2. **Incremental delivery**: each later phase ends at a checkpoint that is independently demonstrable (audit accepted at G-AUDIT, contract layer, remediation per component, test closure, final verification).
3. **Parallel team strategy**: streams ST-INFRA, ST-QA, ST-GOV, ST-REG, ST-SEC, ST-API, ST-WEB, ST-DESK, ST-AND, ST-SUB, ST-DOC, ST-PERF and ST-REV work in parallel once their phase gate opens.

Rev 3 had renumbered after inserting its own tasks and moving the P0 exit gate and the WP-01 register import behind their dependencies.
- Paths, scripts and tool facts proposed by the plan and not yet verified are labelled `(new)` or UNCONFIRMED in the task text; nothing in this list has been executed.
- Every FR (FR-001..FR-025) and SC (SC-001..SC-012) traces to at least one work package in docs/21 section 6; the coverage of this task list against that matrix is checked in the review pass.
